Pitch Playbook. 133 Gonda businesses.

Contacts, outreach plans, automation pitches, demo links — Inexorables Group. Tap a business to expand.

#1 SCPM Super Specialty Hospital & Trauma Center Super-specialty hospital & trauma centre (with nursing/paramedical college group) has website

Contact: Dr. O. N. Pandey (Founder Chairman) — day-to-day decisions likely with Dr. Ayush Pandey / Dr. Ayushi Pandey (Managing Directors) or Mr. Dheeraj Kumar Dubey (Administrator) (Founder Chairman, SCPM Group of Hospital & Education) · +91-9839673554 (also +91-9532745186, +91-9454137268; landline 05262-222583) · WhatsApp

Address: Near Dukh Haran Nath Mandir, Station Road, Gonda, Uttar Pradesh 271003 (college campus: SCPM College Campus, Lucknow Road, Haripur, Gonda 271003)

Demo: open site · own URL

What they do

SCPM Super Specialty Hospital & Trauma Center is the flagship of the SCPM Group of Hospital & Education, run from Station Road near Dukh Haran Nath Mandir in Gonda. It operates as a super-specialty hospital with a dedicated trauma centre and round-the-clock emergency services, reported at roughly 300 beds — by a wide margin the largest private healthcare operation in the district. The hospital was founded on the legacy of Satish Chandra Pandey (the group's namesake) with the original hospital dating to 1990, and it exists to keep serious cases in Gonda instead of sending them down the road to Lucknow. Alongside the hospital, the group runs four teaching institutions on a separate Lucknow Road campus at Haripur: SCPM College of Nursing & Paramedical Sciences (est. 2008), a Pharmacy institute, an Allied Health institute, and an Ayurvedic Medical College (est. 2017) offering BAMS. That gives them two very different customer flows under one brand — patients and emergency cases at Station Road, and admission-seeking students and their parents at Haripur.

Who to contact & how

Dr. O. N. Pandey is the founder and final authority, but he is a senior clinician-owner and hard to get cold. The realistic decision path runs through Dr. Ayush Pandey (Managing Director, the son actively modernising the group) with Mr. Dheeraj Kumar Dubey, the Administrator, as the gatekeeper. Best channel: WhatsApp 9839673554 first with a one-line message and the demo link — this number is published as the group's primary contact and is monitored for admission enquiries, so it gets read. Follow up in person at the Station Road hospital between 11am and 1pm on a weekday, when the administrative office is staffed but OPD peak has passed; ask at the reception counter for "Dubey ji, Administrator" or for the MD's office, and say you already sent the demo on WhatsApp. Avoid morning OPD rush and avoid the Haripur college campus for this conversation — the hospital gap is the pitch, and the hospital office is where it lands. Admission season is when their marketing budget is loosest, but the hospital-site gap is a year-round argument.

Automation pitch

  • A real hospital website separate from the college brochure — departments, trauma/emergency, doctor profiles, an emergency number in the header. Pain: every patient Googling a hospital in Gonda currently reaches a nursing-college page and bounces.
  • WhatsApp OPD appointment booking with automatic confirmation — patient picks department and date, front desk gets a structured message. Pain: appointments today are phone calls to a busy counter, and no-shows are invisible.
  • Lab and radiology reports delivered to WhatsApp — auto-send the moment a report is signed. Pain: families make a second 20km trip just to collect a printout, and reports get lost at the counter.
  • Google-review funnel — an automated post-discharge WhatsApp asking for a review with a one-tap link. Pain: they have 1,600+ reviews and no system; a trickle becomes a moat that no competing Gonda hospital can catch.
  • Admission-enquiry auto-responder for the four colleges — instant reply with course, fee structure and eligibility, then a form that lands in a sheet. Pain: enquiry calls spike in season and get missed, and every missed call is a lost seat.
  • Follow-up and admission/discharge reminders — automated nudges for post-op reviews, dialysis and dressing schedules, and pending bill/fee instalments. Pain: follow-ups depend on the patient remembering, so revenue and outcomes both leak.

Opening line

"Sir, I searched for a hospital in Gonda and your site came up — but it's a nursing-college page; the About section still has demo text from the theme on it, and there's nothing about the trauma centre or your departments anywhere. So I built you a working demo of what a hospital site for SCPM should look like — appointment booking, reports going straight to WhatsApp, emergency number right at the top. It's live, it took me a day, and there's no charge to look at it. May I show you on your phone?"

Automation pitch

  • A real contact layer on the website — click-to-call, click-to-WhatsApp and an email address on every page — pain: their contact page today publishes no phone, no email and no address, so a ready-to-book guest hits a dead end.

Demo: open site · own URL

What they do

New Gupta Electronics is a multi-brand electronics and home-appliance showroom on the Gonda–Utraula Road, trading since 1986 under proprietor Shashank Gupta. The main store carries televisions, refrigerators, washing machines, air conditioners, coolers and kitchen appliances, with a strong LG focus — the family also runs a dedicated LG Best Shop franchise outlet at LIC City Center. A third arm, the Sleepwell Gallery, is Sleepwell's exclusive brand gallery for Gonda, on Ayodhya Road opposite the LIC office, selling mattresses, pillows, sofa-cum-beds and furnishing. Between the three outlets they cover almost the whole "setting up a home" purchase — screen, fridge, AC and the bed you sleep on. They sell on GST bills with manufacturer warranty, run EMI/consumer-finance options, and deliver and install across Gonda district. Reviewers routinely describe the store as the best-known electronics shop in Gonda and the Devipatan Mandal region.

Who to contact & how

Shashank Gupta, Proprietor — he is the decision-maker and, based on the review replies posted under the business, he personally handles the online side. No committee, no manager to route through.

Best channel: walk in at the main Utraula Road (Patel Nagar) showroom, 11am–1pm, before the afternoon rush and after the morning opening. Ask at the counter for "Shashank Gupta ji" by name — using the name signals you did homework and gets you past the salesman. If told he is at the Sleepwell Gallery on Ayodhya Road, go there directly rather than leaving a message; it is a short hop across town.

Alternative/warm-up: WhatsApp +91 99180 09000 with one line and the demo link — "Sir, I built a sample website for New Gupta Electronics, no charge to look at it" — then follow up in person the next day. Do not open with price.

Opening hook that will land: he already replies to Google reviews, so he understands the online channel. The gap is that all that effort currently sends customers to Sleepwell's corporate page instead of his own.

Automation pitch

  • WhatsApp price & EMI auto-responder — customers currently phone to ask "TV ka rate kya hai, EMI kitni banegi"; every call ties up a salesman during peak hours. An auto-reply captures the enquiry, sends the model list and EMI slabs, and hands the counter a warm lead instead of an interruption.
  • Own website that consolidates all three outlets — right now electronics, LG Best Shop and the Sleepwell Gallery look like three unrelated businesses online. One site under their own name makes a customer buying a mattress discover they also sell the fridge.
  • Broadcast catalogue for festival & brand offers — Diwali/Dhanteras schemes, LG exchange offers and Sleepwell promos go out to past customers as an image + price list on WhatsApp instead of relying on a Facebook post nobody sees.
  • Google-review funnel — they already earn reviews organically; an automated post-delivery WhatsApp message with the review link compounds a lead they are already winning, and pushes all three profiles higher in local search.
  • Delivery & installation status updates — "your AC will be installed today between 2 and 4 pm" sent automatically ends the follow-up calls their staff currently field all day.
  • Service-request intake — a warranty or service complaint captured on WhatsApp with model and bill photo, logged and tracked, so nothing is lost on a paper slip and the after-sales reputation stays intact.

Opening line

"Gupta ji, namaste — I looked up New Gupta Electronics online. You've got over a thousand five-star reviews on the Sleepwell Gallery alone, more than any shop in Gonda, but when someone clicks your website link they land on Sleepwell's own page — not yours. Nothing about the LG Best Shop, nothing about forty years of your name. So I built you a sample site — here, on my phone — that puts all three of your showrooms in one place with a WhatsApp price-and-EMI enquiry button. Take two minutes and look; if it's not useful, I'll leave it there."

What they do

Fatima Senior Secondary School is Gonda's flagship English-medium CBSE school — a co-educational senior secondary institution on Circular Road, Bargaon, run by the Catholic Diocese of Lucknow and operating since 1976 (the Principal's own message on their site refers to "the history of forty-eight years", written in 2024). It is CBSE-affiliated under affiliation number 2130053, school code 70475, and teaches from the junior classes (LKG intake with four named sections — Bright, Clever, Smart, Brave) up through Class XII. The campus carries a full facility set the school documents publicly: science and computer labs, library, smart classrooms, audio-visual room, sports grounds, art & craft, music & dance, cafeteria (a second cafeteria block of 374 m² was added recently), health care, hostel, and picnic/education outings. Its network infrastructure is unusually serious for the district: fibre backbone, Cisco routing and switching, a dedicated 1 Gbps line, and Wi-Fi across both the academic block and the hostel. Who to contact & how

Automation pitch

  • WhatsApp admission-enquiry auto-responder on 9151324447. Pain: their online admission module is switched off, so every enquiry becomes a phone call or a trip to the office; a bot answers class availability, dates, document lists and fee basics at 10 PM on a Sunday.
  • Digital LKG registration form with dated slot booking. Pain: parents currently hand-carry printed forms to a five-day window, 10 AM–2 PM, and the office runs a physical queue for the district's most contested seats — an online form with an auto-assigned submission slot removes the queue and the "forfeit your seat" phone calls.
  • Fee due-date and instalment reminders on WhatsApp. Pain: fees are already collected online ("Online Payment — Click Here to Pay") but chasing is manual; automatic reminders before each instalment cut both defaults and the follow-up calls the office makes.
  • Result and notice broadcast to parent groups. Pain: they publish board results and circulars to a website almost nobody checks and a notice feed with one entry — the same content pushed to every parent's WhatsApp reaches the audience that actually matters.
  • Transport / van-route alerts for the 4-van fleet. Pain: the school's own transport rules put the burden on parents to have children at the pick-up point on time, with "the transport will not be delayed" — automated delay and arrival alerts end the daily gate-side uncertainty.
  • A public-facing site that search engines can actually read, with maps and social wired in. Pain: the current SPA serves an empty HTML page, has no map link and no Facebook/Instagram links, so a fifty-year-old brand is invisible to anyone searching "best CBSE school in Gonda".
  • Google-review funnel from the parent list. Pain: 129 Justdial ratings for the district's most reputed school is under-representation; a post-result, post-annual-day nudge converts goodwill into visible reviews.

Automation pitch

Frame this correctly before anything else. Yashmay is the one business on the Gonda list that does not need a website. Theirs is genuinely the best site in the district — a real agency build, ~27 interior pages, an online admission form, an ERP parent login, five social channels. Say so, out loud, first. The sale here is a layer that sits on top of what they already own: the site collects the enquiry beautifully and then nothing happens to it for eighteen hours. We sell the eighteen hours.

Sequence the pitch in this order — it moves from their strongest asset to the gap, and every item plugs into the existing site rather than replacing it:

1. WhatsApp reply on the enquiry their site already captures. Their inquiry form works; what is missing is the answer. A parent submits at 9 PM and gets the fee sheet, boarding options and the next three campus-tour slots on WhatsApp within seconds. Nothing about the site changes — we hang a responder off the form they already have. *Solves: enquiries won by whoever replies first, which today is never them at night.*

2. A follow-up sequence on day 1 / day 3 / day 7. Brochure, then a campus-tour invitation, then a board-results one-pager. A ₹2-lakh decision is made over weeks, and right now every one of those weeks is manual. *Solves: enquiries that go cold with nobody assigned to them.*

3. Campus-tour self-booking with a morning-of reminder. They are a gated 25-acre campus — a visit is the whole sale. Let the parent pick the slot and remind them the morning of. *Solves: no-shows and the admissions desk playing phone-tag.*

4. Fee due-date and instalment reminders, per student, on WhatsApp. This one runs off their EduPage data, not off the website at all — so it is additive to both vendors. *Solves: collection chasing by phone at a premium price point, which strains the relationship it is trying to protect.*

5. Parent broadcasts to the right class group. Holidays, exam schedules, result announcements, bus and boarding updates — automatic, targeted, no diary. *Solves: notices that reach the parents who happened to check.*

6. Google-review funnel after PTM and results. Edustoke shows a single vote for the district's most expensive school. Every strong claim on their site is self-published; a parent Googling "best school in Gonda" finds no parent voices. One-tap review links turn satisfied families into the proof layer their marketing is currently missing. *Solves: thin third-party proof — the one weakness their excellent website cannot fix by itself.*

7. One-number consolidation and a filled Google Business Profile. A single WhatsApp Business number, and the same street address and email across the site, EduPage, Facebook and Google. Cheap, fast, and it is the fix that makes everything above measurable. *Solves: fragmented contact data and weak local-search grounding leaking walk-ins today.*

Anchor everything to the demo page: it exists to show the WhatsApp funnel working end to end, not to propose a redesign. If they ask whether we are replacing their site, the answer is no — and say it before they have to ask.

  • IVF and surgery follow-up sequences. IVF and post-operative care are long, multi-visit journeys where a missed follow-up is a clinical risk, not just a billing one. Scheduled WhatsApp check-ins keep the patient on protocol without the staff tracking it manually.
  • Google-review funnel triggered after discharge. A hospital with nine specialities and near-zero visible review volume is invisible in "hospital near me" searches. One automated post-visit message asking for a review, sent to the right patients, compounds into local search rank.
  • Consultant-roster page that the staff can update themselves. The site cannot answer "which specialist, which day" because updating it requires the developer. A self-serve roster the front desk edits weekly removes both the phone calls and the dependence on whoever built the site.
  • Immediate cleanup of the two live defects. Delete the "Hello world!" placeholder and the duplicate /home-copy/ page, and put address, phone and hours in the homepage header. This costs nothing and is the credibility proof that opens the larger conversation.

Opening line

Implant, braces and RCT enquiries carry very different values and prep; auto-tagging them by service on arrival lets the doctor prioritise high-value consultations. Pain: high-value implant enquiries currently sit in the same undifferentiated pile as a filling query.
  • Live "24-hour telephonic consultation" button. They already advertise it — turn it into a one-tap call/WhatsApp CTA that works at 11pm. Pain: the promise exists on the page with no way to act on it, which erodes rather than builds trust.
  • Post-treatment follow-up and review capture. An automatic message a day after an extraction or RCT (aftercare instructions plus a review request). Pain: no visible review presence at all, so the clinic's reputation is invisible to anyone searching Gonda dentists.

Opening line

"Your site says you do implants, braces and root canals — but it doesn't tell a patient a single doctor's name, and the email on it is masked. I've built you a version that fixes both, and books appointments straight to WhatsApp. Two minutes on your phone?"
